# ZigCSS Development Status

Last updated: 2026-07-25

## Execution context

- Goal: complete the approved roadmap in `DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md`.
- Branch: `vale/zigcss-recovery`
- Base commit: `2d2c0d90208610e1e7a623185372ddeab01fc44c` (`main` and `origin/main` at autonomous start)
- Verified execution model: `gpt-5.6-sol`, ultra reasoning, as explicitly configured and approved in the autonomous-development handoff.
- Worktree: isolated Codex worktree `/Users/vyakymenko/.codex/worktrees/9302/zigcss`; the user's main checkout is out of scope.
- Inherited changes preserved: the approved `DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md` was untracked at autonomous start, was committed as the authoritative roadmap, and has since changed only through recorded owner-approved amendments and implementation evidence.
- External actions: on 2026-07-13 the operator explicitly authorized automatic non-force pushes of each clean green checkpoint to `origin` at the same current `vale/*` branch, then explicitly authorized merging that branch to `origin/main` without a pull request. On 2026-07-14 the operator authorized completing the remaining external gates; workflow-based GitHub Pages was enabled and its existing deployment workflow was dispatched on exact integrated `main`. On 2026-07-15 the operator explicitly resumed the remaining unproven work, including published packages and release artifacts. On 2026-07-16 the operator instructed continuation after the failed public `v0.4.0-rc.1` tag could no longer be moved safely, so the release advanced to `0.4.0-rc.2` instead of rewriting public history. Its guarded run also failed closed before publication, so continued autonomous remediation advanced to the published `0.4.0-rc.3`; neither immutable failed tag will be moved. On 2026-07-16 the operator explicitly funded full canonical SCSS, indented Sass, Less, and Stylus integration and instructed that it be added to autonomous development. On 2026-07-17 the operator authorized running the prepared release, but before any tag was created superseded that direction with a self-contained native/zero-production-dependency requirement. The `0.5.0-rc.1` publication was cancelled with no tag, release, attestation, package, or artifact created; ADR-013 and Milestone 10 now govern the product. Automatic green checkpoint push and non-force main integration remain authorized. A future native release still requires its fail-closed graduation gate and separate publication authorization.

## Current work

- Milestone: Milestone 10 self-contained native stylesheet frontends, in parallel with Milestone 8 controlled benchmark evidence
- Work package: `NSASS-011` native Sass evaluation
- State: `IN_PROGRESS`
- Active phase: the eighty-seventh native Sass semantic checkpoint, `NSASS-011-META-MATH-UNARY-FUNCTION-INVOCATION`, is `VERIFIED` at local implementation checkpoint `798a3d8359b539c69abc4b396cf9c019fc9a6752`. Provider-free native Zig now invokes the exact already-admitted unary `sass:math` function-reference family through `meta.call()` for SCSS and indented Sass: `abs`, `ceil`, `floor`, `round`, and `percentage`. Default, exact custom, and unprefixed module access retain module ownership without a legacy-global warning, while true legacy-global references emit the existing normalized warning. Canonical positional and named `$number` binding, list/map splats, numeric units, percentage conversion, and exact once-only source order agree with Dart Sass 1.101.0. Missing, extra, unknown, duplicate, non-number, and unitful-percentage arguments fail closed, while an explicit `math.compatible` reference keeps every remaining unmeasured callable family outside this slice. Fresh oracle probes reproduce exact compressed SCSS `.values{abs:1.5px;ceil:2px;floor:-2px;round-list:-1px;percentage-map:12.5%;ordered:-2foo;trace:12}`, indented Sass `.sass{abs:1.5px;named:-2deg}`, legacy `.legacy{abs:1px;ceil:2px;floor:1px;round:2px;percentage:12.5%}` plus five warnings, and all selected invalid bindings. The test-first focused Debug gate stopped only at the old dispatch boundary with 307/310 tests. Final direct evaluator coverage passes 177/177; focused native Debug/ReleaseSafe each pass 23/23 steps with 310/310 tests; full compiler Debug/ReleaseSafe each pass 60/60 steps with 805/805 tests; and direct evaluator no-emission cross-compiles pass for Windows x64 GNU and Linux ARM64 GNU in both modes. Native contract 10/10 plus check adds only `built-in-meta-math-unary-function-invocation` while retaining private native Sass rows, production unreachability, zero target package/optional/runtime dependencies, and the closed release interlock. Complete provider/reference/product matrices pass 151/151, preprocessor package policy passes 6/6 plus check, autonomous-runner selftest passes 58/58, and repository Zig formatting, JavaScript/JSON syntax, and diff checks pass. The temporary isolated macOS 15.4 SDK selector required by pinned Zig 0.15.2 was removed after validation; no tracked or system state changed. Production routing, public capability claims, native graduation, version, release interlock, tag, artifact/package publication, deployment, and every external system remain unchanged.
- Quality closure: `QUAL-001` is verified at `3eced86`; repository-wide Zig formatting debt is zero and the final local validation matrix is green
- Next eligible package: measure and select the smallest next unsupported dependency-ordered `NSASS-011` Sass semantic slice. Do not infer three or more legacy `if()` splats, nonterminal dual-splat recovery, modern CSS conditional evaluation, plain-CSS function references, remaining meta/math/selector/string/color/calculation/special-function reference invocation including the explicit `math.compatible` boundary, mixin application, module enumeration, dynamic loading, user-module reflection, imports/forwards, direct callable CSS serialization, broader direct-call variadic keyword behavior, or normalized diagnostics beyond executable evidence. Provider removal and the package-level zero-dependency cutover remain gated on native graduation rather than deleting the exact development oracles early. Independently, `BENCH-007` still requires a dedicated non-emulated Linux x64 runner with labels `[self-hosted, linux, x64, zigcss-benchmark-v1]`; its absence must not pause eligible native frontend work.

## Baseline commands and results

Baseline captured on base commit `2d2c0d9` with Zig 0.15.2, Node 24.16.0, and npm 11.13.0. The host is macOS 26.5; Zig 0.15.2's bundled linker could not consume the macOS 26.5 SDK stubs, so native commands used an isolated `xcrun` wrapper pointing at the already-installed macOS 15.4 SDK plus `MACOSX_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=15.4`. A fresh-cache retry against the 26.5 SDK reproduced the linker failure, while the 15.4 SDK completed unchanged builds and tests.

| Surface | Command | Result |
|---|---|---|
| Zig Debug build | `zig build --summary all` | `PASS`; 3/3 steps succeeded. |
| Zig ReleaseSafe build | `zig build -Doptimize=ReleaseSafe --summary all` | `PASS`; 3/3 steps succeeded. |
| Zig tests | `zig build test --summary all` | `PASS`; 76/76 tests. |
| Zig formatting | `zig fmt --check build.zig build_helpers.zig src examples` | `FAIL`; 19 inherited `.zig` files require formatting. No bulk formatting was applied because it would mix unrelated churn into containment packages. |
| Docs tests | `cd docs && npm run test:run` | `PASS`; 6 files and 37 tests. |
| Docs build | `cd docs && npm run build` | `PASS`; Vite emitted `docs/dist`. |
| Docs dependency install/audit | `cd docs && npm ci --ignore-scripts` | Install passed; npm reported 10 advisories: 1 low, 2 moderate, 6 high, 1 critical. Resolution belongs to `DEP-001`. |
| npm package dry run | `npm pack --dry-run --ignore-scripts` | `PASS`; package contains 5 files, 53.0 kB unpacked. |
| Audit reproductions | Temporary corpus and live docs-server requests | `FAIL` as expected; concrete evidence below. |

The approved repository allowlist, fixed model/effort, clean-tree requirement, timeout, no-publication boundary, and all prior confinement controls remain intact. Fifty-eight hermetic assertions include exact native prompt-order guards, stale-prompt rejection, successful dual-ref integration, diverged-main rejection with atomic no-partial-update evidence, and state readback. Bash syntax, ShellCheck when available, documentation 7/7 plus check, and diff checks pass. Exact-main Build `30103254468` on `0f8b9dad50da18d9e1e68484a0d3350079812d9a` passed Test Suite `89514325720` and all five platform archive/offline-install jobs. A clean live restart remains the operator-side activation step. | `0f8b9da` |

## Foundational ADR ledger

| Decision | State | Evidence / decision | Commit |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ADR-001` | `VERIFIED` | Stable scope is standards-oriented CSS library/CLI parsing, diagnostics, spans, deterministic emission, and only evidence-backed transforms; ecosystem adapters remain explicit experiments. | `cd5c45f` |
| `ADR-002` | `VERIFIED` | CSS Syntax algorithms define token behavior; original byte spans, retained trivia, lossless nested component values, recoverable diagnostics, and transform-free syntax boundaries are mandatory. | `cd5c45f` |
| `ADR-003` | `VERIFIED` | A per-compilation arena owns sources through AST; returned CSS/maps/diagnostics have independent result ownership and one cleanup path with allocation-failure coverage. | `cd5c45f` |
| `ADR-004` | `VERIFIED` | Transform passes use immutable root handoff, exact deny-by-default safety classes, deterministic dependency plans, compilation-arena outputs, scratch validation storage, explicit acceptance evidence, and transactional root/diagnostic installation. | `e8176cb` |
| `ADR-005` | `VERIFIED` | Every inherited alternate-format adapter remains unavailable until strict evidence exists. SCSS/Sass, Less, Stylus, CSS-in-JS, PostCSS-like, and Tailwind-like behavior use `remove-until-funded`; CSS Modules alone may pursue a closed `limited-native-subset`. Unsupported syntax must diagnose without partial CSS, and transformed bytes may not enter the inherited parser. | `90d51a1` |
| `ADR-006` | `VERIFIED` | Browser compatibility uses a closed deterministic target grammar and pinned generated BCD data; qualifiers, gaps, partial support, and unsupported forms remain explicit rather than guessed. | `84fa0e6` |
| `ADR-007` | `VERIFIED` | Generated syntax carries an explicit causal source span; only its first generated byte maps to the authored construct start, no false inner precision or offset-zero fallback is allowed, and generated payloads require structured closed serialization. | `249632f` |
| `ADR-010` | `VERIFIED` | The configured `gpt-5.6-sol` ultra runtime and one implementation agent are hard gates; fallback models and delegated implementation are prohibited. | `cd5c45f` |
| `ADR-011` | `VERIFIED` | Native plugins are a trusted Zig-only experimental source API, not a sandbox or stable ABI. Definitions and user state are borrowed synchronously, IDs stay in an isolated `plugin.` namespace, execution reuses bounded deterministic pass plans, and configuration/execution failures preserve compiler transaction boundaries while caller-owned side effects remain the plugin's responsibility. Renumbered from the provisional ADR-005 identifier when Milestone 5 activated the roadmap-reserved preprocessor ADR. | `05879af`, `90d51a1` |

## Milestone 3 exit validation

Milestone 3 is `PASS` at gate checkpoint `89417a8`.

| Gate | Result |
|---|---|
| Package closure | `PASS`; `PASS-001`, `OPT-010` through `OPT-015`, `VAL-001`, `MATH-001`, `CUSTOM-001`, `LOGICAL-001`, `PREFIX-001`, `PREFIX-002`, and `TREE-001` are verified and checkpointed. |
| Stable optimizer admission | `PASS`; `--optimize` invokes one static `verified-optimizer` registry containing exactly seven verified definitions: duplicate analysis, empty cleanup, numeric math folding, typed color/zero shortening, margin shorthand synthesis, adjacent at-rule merge, and adjacent selector-rule merge. Policy grants only lossless cleanup and semantic rewriting. Compatibility, extraction, experimental maturity, proven reorder, static custom-property resolution, and logical-to-physical conversion have no registry path. The inherited optimizer and legacy code generator remain unreachable. |
| Cross-pass fixed point | `PASS`; proof-carrying nodes cannot silently defer a second safe candidate to a later CLI invocation. The preset runs no more than 32 parse-transform-emit rounds and succeeds only when consecutive bytes match. Tests cover cleanup→merge, math→zero, math→shorthand, value→selector merge, and at-rule→inner-selector merge; every allocation failure is handled and non-convergence produces no output. Fixed-point source maps remain explicitly unavailable pending composed-map evidence. |
| Source order and nesting | `PASS`; rebuilt transform and prefix sources contain no `swapRemove`; all admitted passes are recursively nested and order-preserving by validated metadata. CLI target contracts preserve importance/fallback chains, non-adjacent boundaries, custom-property cascade, logical RTL/vertical names and values, unsupported selectors/values, group/layer contexts, and batch determinism. Legacy `src/optimizer.zig` still contains order-unstable operations but is not imported by the preset. |
| Browser targets and diagnostics | `PASS`; the reviewed legacy query materially expands the pinned eight-feature fixture from 591 to 2,111 minified bytes, while modern Chrome/Edge/Firefox is the exact transform-free root. Dynamic/unknown target input fails at its byte offset without CSS, and target rewriting remains separately authorized rather than part of `--optimize`. |
| Independent semantics and size | `PASS`; Lightning CSS 1.30.1 independently canonicalizes all ten reviewed pretty/minified transform fixtures, projects target-only compatibility forms and both selector-extraction subsets, checks exact goldens/order, rejects bad query/inventory input, and proves reparse byte stability. The combined optimizer fixture preserves cascade-sensitive boundaries and falls from 1,143 to 866 minified bytes. The grammar matrix remains 21 features, 18 independently parsed outputs, and four deterministic rejections. |
| Debug / ReleaseSafe | `PASS`; each completes 13/13 test steps and 425/425 tests (80 legacy unit, 310 library/core, 35 CLI integration), plus 3/3 build steps. The preset registry, dependency closure, fixed-point execution, batch/single CLI paths, every allocation failure, parser/emitter equivalence, maps for ordinary single plans, and all prior regressions pass. |
| Generated data / docs / packaging | `PASS`; four prefix-data generator tests and byte-exact BCD 8.0.0 regeneration pass; 82/82 docs tests and the Vite build pass; benchmark smoke completes; root production audit reports zero vulnerabilities; npm dry-run remains five files and 14,517 bytes unpacked. |
| Formatting and determinism | `PASS` for every M3-owned clean-format file, JavaScript and shell syntax, generated bytes, and `git diff --check`. Repository-wide checking reports exactly the same 19 inherited Zig files recorded at baseline; no formatting-only churn was included. |

A dedicated `compileBatch` scope now unwinds every task before main maps exit 1/2, including output-collision and planning failures. Only a joined all-success state reaches writes; destinations and `Compiled:` status lines are committed in original argument order, and failure diagnostics are rendered in that same stable order. Per-destination atomicity retains CLI-012's explicit non-transactional operational-write boundary. Unit tests prove the cap, claim/cancel state machine, distinct allocator contexts, leak-free failed-batch unwind, and no output on failure. A ten-input child regression exceeds the cap and exact-checks all CSS plus status order; another uses seven slow in-flight inputs to prove a missing ninth input remains unclaimed after the first parser failure and no output directory is created. ## Milestone 7 exit validation

Milestone 7 implementation criteria remain `PASS`; `REL-006` is implemented at `a0115c5` and awaits its authorized hosted publication evidence. The public compile service remains disabled under the verified `SEC-002` containment decision, so service re-enablement packages `DOC-003` and `WEB-001` remain deferred until the full request/process isolation dependency is implemented; no disabled route is treated as a release capability.

| Gate | Result |
|---|---|
| Documentation truthfulness and integrity | `PASS`; generated capability metadata drives both Markdown tables and the site, 126/126 docs tests and the production build pass, and seven integrity tests cover 31 Markdown files, 60 fences/50 executable, 41 links, and 24 routes. |
| Dependency security | `PASS`; exact lock/manifest policy and live production audits report 0/0/0 vulnerabilities across root, docs, and VS Code, including zero high or critical findings. |
| Release identity and trust metadata | `PASS`; version/tag checks, five target-specific archives, SHA-256 manifests, deterministic SPDX 2.3 SBOMs, local Sigstore binding, and mandatory GitHub cryptographic verification remain fail-closed before release creation. |
| Consumer and installation paths | `PASS`; npm, Docker, and Homebrew trust boundaries retain 16 hermetic regressions and their live host evidence. Every tag target now additionally requires direct archive execution and a real offline npm pack/postinstall/wrapper lifecycle on a matching runner. |
| Workflow authority | `PASS`; four workflows, nine least-privilege jobs, and 28 immutable action references pass the closed policy. Release creation alone owns repository-content write; npm publication alone owns package provenance OIDC; no model-pass publication path exists. |
| Compiler regression surface | `PASS`; Debug and ReleaseSafe each complete 34/34 steps and 489/489 tests. Production Zig code is unchanged by `REL-005`. |
| Publication boundary | `PASS`; npm authority and immutable-version availability must pass before native work, and all five release jobs must finish metadata, native smoke, attestation, local binding, and cryptographic verification before GitHub release creation and exact `next`-channel provenance publication become reachable. A source checkout does not claim that hosted publication completed; registry and release evidence must be recorded after the tag run. |

The complete benchmark chain, compatibility, ten transform, eight-format, prefix, package/consumer/example, wrapper, version, release-metadata, dependency, capability, workflow, documentation, syntax, and diff gates pass. Docs remain 126/126 plus build and 31-file/60-fence/42-link/24-route integrity; production audits are 0/0/0; capability metadata is 21 rows/19 gates; npm is five files/45.8 kB; formatting debt remains ten inherited Zig files. No real scheduled artifact exists or was dispatched, so no timing, memory, throughput, ranking, ratio, archive, or public result was selected; final verification requires that external controlled input. | `509e2ef` |

## Milestone 8 activation boundary

Milestone 8 remains `IN_PROGRESS` at implementation checkpoint `509e2ef`. `BENCH-001` through `BENCH-006` are verified, and the complete `BENCH-007` publication implementation is green. The repository deliberately remains in the exact withdrawn state because a synthetic or manually invented archive cannot satisfy the roadmap's controlled scheduled evidence requirement. The first eligible archive must be produced by the schedule-only workflow on integrated upstream `main`, retained and reviewed, then committed and selected under the new fail-closed publication contract before `BENCH-007`, Milestone 8, or the autonomous program can become `VERIFIED`/`PASS`/complete.

## Completed work packages

- `SEC-001`: static serving is contained within the configured real root and malformed URL encoding is handled without a process crash. Focused result: 7/7 security tests; integration result: 44/44 docs tests plus successful Vite build.
- `SEC-002` containment decision: the public compile service is disabled rather than exposed without its required resource and isolation limits. Re-enablement requires the full package gates.
- `TEST-001`: the audit corpus runs the actual CLI as a child process, safely captures crash signatures, and maps every quarantine to the package that must replace it with a target-contract assertion.
- `OPT-001`: legacy code generation no longer invokes the inherited optimizer and still rejects every transform-bearing field with `UnsafeTransformsDisabled`. The rebuilt CLI now owns its separate acceptance-gated preset; minification remains an independent emission mode.
- `PROF-001`: explicit and deferred `end()` calls are safe; profiler cleanup no longer double-frees timing names.
- `CLI-001`: every planned output is checked against every input and prior output before directory creation or compilation; rejected plans preserve all sources.
- `CLI-002`: the CLI accepts only functional recovery-scope options. Source maps, target/prefix queries, and extraction modes still fail before reading input; Milestone 3 separately admits only the closed verified `--optimize` preset.
- `CI-001`: documentation validation and deployment are separate jobs; pull requests cannot execute the deploy job and the uploaded artifact matches Vite's verified output directory.
- `CI-002`: target builds happen after native tests so tests cannot replace cross artifacts; a shared tested inspector validates architecture and executable format before upload or release archive creation.
- `SEC-003`: the public docs runtime is static-only, non-root, high-port, and root-owned; a filtered build context excludes repository metadata, generated output, dependency trees, and environment files.
- `SAFE-001`: the recovery CLI emits an experimental warning and rejects legacy format adapters; active public documentation is limited to the tested status, source-build, and CLI contracts, while prior performance comparisons are explicitly invalidated.
- `TOK-002`: semantic token values decode CSS escapes on demand while raw original spelling remains source-addressable; URL and malformed-token recovery always terminates, and unicode-range recognition is enabled only for its descriptor-specific entry point.
- `TOK-003`: lossless consumers receive comment and whitespace tokens instead of implicit gaps; normalized consumers can discard both through `Token.isTrivia()`, while unterminated-comment metadata remains available for later structured diagnostics.
- `MEM-001`: compilation-lifetime objects allocate from one arena, while result-lifetime CSS, maps, and diagnostic messages are deep-copied into a recorded result allocator and released only through `CompileResult.deinit`.
- `SYN-001`: component values retain every top-level source byte while representing nested blocks and functions explicitly; missing closers remain `null` metadata for recovery/diagnostics instead of being synthesized into the source model.
- `DIAG-001`: CSS parse problems are structured values on `Compilation.diagnostics`; only allocator exhaustion, unknown sources, and explicit resource limits leave the parse API as operational errors.
- `AST-001`: the new standards-oriented selector AST represents adjacency inside a compound separately from explicit combinators between compounds; typed pseudo arguments never replace their lossless component-value backing.
- `AST-002`: declaration values retain the complete raw component stream (including `!important` and trivia); `ImportantAnnotation.value_end` identifies the semantic prefix without deleting spelling or collapsing duplicate declarations.
- `AST-003`: at-rule block interpretation is a tagged choice rather than a universal rule/declaration assumption; every category retains the original prelude and exact brace/EOF envelope.
- `PAR-001`: selector lowering follows the current CSSWG Selectors Level 4 Editor's Draft grammar; style-rule lists are strict, only `:is()`/`:where()` are forgiving, and their discarded items do not create compilation errors.
- `PAR-002`: declaration parsing never searches raw delimiters inside nested syntax; only top-level semicolon component values synchronize candidates, and malformed candidates remain source-preserved gaps in the ordered declaration-list span.
- `PAR-003`: rule parsing recognizes only top-level semicolon and curly-block component boundaries; qualified rules compose the typed selector/declaration parsers, while each at-rule stores a decoded name, exact raw prelude, and explicit body category.
- `PAR-004`: structured at-rule details are additive views over the generic lossless at-rule. Typed lowering never deletes raw preludes/bodies; invalid typed syntax leaves `details` null or skips only the invalid keyframe/page child while retaining source-addressable raw syntax and diagnostics.
- `ERR-001`: lowering synchronizes only at top-level component-value boundaries, so nested delimiters cannot split an outer rule. Precise nested diagnostics suppress generic parent fallbacks, while matching syntax containers consume their own closers and any visible stray closer is isolated before the next rule.
- `EMIT-001`: pretty emission is structural where the grammar is typed and lossless where values/unknown at-rules remain component streams. It canonicalizes identifiers and strings, never invokes transforms, preserves declaration/rule order, and returns `UnrepresentableRecovery` or `UnterminatedSyntax` whenever formatting would otherwise discard or synthesize meaningful source.
- `EMIT-002`: minification is aggressive only across typed structural grammar. Raw component streams conservatively collapse existing whitespace runs to one token instead of guessing that whitespace is semantically removable; this protects custom syntax, descendant selectors in raw pseudo arguments, list separation, and whitespace-sensitive operators while still removing all structural formatting.
- `ROUND-001`: structural equivalence compares semantic tags, decoded values, ordered children, and normalized component streams rather than source spans or output text. Untyped raw syntax retains leading/trailing whitespace-token presence recursively; typed grammar may normalize representational edge trivia without changing equivalence.
- `MAP-001`: source maps are emitted as separate owned artifacts; CSS bytes never receive an implicit source-map comment. Mappings are captured at write time from generated UTF-16 positions to original span starts, use source index zero for the compilation source, and refine duplicate generated starts to the most specific later node.
- `NEST-001`: style-rule block contents follow declaration-first disambiguation without flattening cascade order. The initial declaration run remains on the style rule; every later run is an ordered nested-declarations child. Nested group rules use the same block-content algorithm, and emitter modes preserve that sequence while omitting only block-final declaration semicolons.
- `M2-CLI`: stable CSS compilation enters through `css/pipeline.zig`; it never constructs the inherited AST, calls the inherited parser, or invokes inherited code generation. Stable CLI source-map flags remain rejected until a file/comment output contract is specified even though the library pipeline can return a separately owned map.
- `M2-COMPAT`: the public Markdown matrix and machine-readable fixture manifest are cross-checked, while the independent parser consumes actual stable-CLI output rather than fixture input. Validator recovery is forbidden so acceptance cannot come from silently discarding malformed output.
- `PASS-001`: plans borrow pass definitions but own deterministic ordered pointer slices. Output-changing passes reconstruct const AST paths in the compilation arena; `ParsedStylesheet.applyPassPlan` installs a root only after the full plan succeeds, while failed candidates and diagnostic side effects remain unobservable.
- `OPT-010`: cleanup eligibility is intentionally closed: empty style rules and empty `media`, `supports`, `container`, or `starting-style` groups only. Empty layer/scope/keyframe/document/descriptor/page/unknown rules are preserved, as are comments that separate value tokens and all ordered declarations.
- `OPT-011`: duplicate declarations are exposed as owned, bounded analysis facts only. Standard names are ASCII case-insensitive, custom-property names are case-sensitive, list segments are hard scope boundaries, and importance transitions/fallback chains do not imply deletion safety.
- `VAL-001`: numeric interpretation is an immutable typed view over lossless component values. It retains source envelopes and context-relative units, reports invalid dimension algebra without rewriting syntax, and by itself grants no folding or shortening authority.
- `MATH-001`: numeric folding is an explicitly authorized semantic rewrite over a closed property/category policy. It retains one exact unit, declines context-dependent conversion and incomplete evidence, and emits only structured finite numerics when strictly shorter; the stable CLI reaches it only through the closed accepted preset.
- `OPT-012`: color typing is limited to exact 8-bit sRGB evidence, and unitless zero is limited to positive zero lengths on grammars without a competing number interpretation. Shortening never raises alpha-hex compatibility requirements without explicit target data.
- `OPT-013`: shorthand synthesis is limited to one exact safe token repeated across the canonical adjacent physical margin reset set. Authored declarations remain as proof; page and page-margin descriptor contexts stay unchanged because the current independent oracle cannot prove their shorthand equivalence.
- `OPT-014`: selector-rule merging is the reverse of expanding one selector list into adjacent identical declaration blocks. It never crosses a rule-list, at-rule, omission, recovery, or nesting boundary; blocks containing child rules are excluded so merging cannot change the parent selector list used by a descendant `&`. Representation-only declaration differences may merge only after typed component comparison, while distinct comments and custom-property token boundaries remain authored.
- `OPT-015`: typed group merging retains one identical conditional or named-layer context and concatenates only its two adjacent child streams. It never crosses a parent rule-list, omission, recovery, anonymous-layer identity, different query, or nesting boundary. Direct nested-declaration streams remain authored because Lightning CSS 1.30.1 cannot currently provide a sound independent canonical oracle for that shape; nested style and generated-proof streams remain eligible and ordered.
- `CUSTOM-001`: custom-property definitions and `var()` consumers are authored protected values on every default rebuilt value/declaration traversal. General static evaluation is not a source-only optimization because names are case-sensitive and substitution depends on element cascade, inheritance, fallbacks, registrations, and computed-value cycle handling. Any future mode must be a separate experimental value pass with three explicit policy grants and new DOM/cascade evidence; it cannot hitchhike on a verified pass or the stable CLI.
- `LOGICAL-001`: no logical-to-physical conversion mode is retained. Physical mapping requires used writing-mode, direction, text-orientation, the controlling box, inheritance, and the ordered cascade between logical and physical declarations; a stylesheet-global target is not sufficient. The reserved metadata effect is rejected at registry validation, so a future proposal requires a new package, context model, computed-style oracle, and ADR revision.
- `PREFIX-001`: target queries are a small deterministic ZigCSS language, not a partial Browserslist implementation. A fixed BCD release supplies reviewed facts rather than ambient policy; partial support and upstream-note presence remain conservative result qualifiers. No compatibility rewrite or stable CLI flag is enabled until `PREFIX-002` proves target-dependent syntax, ordering, idempotence, source maps, and independent semantic validation.
- `PREFIX-002`: compatibility generation is a closed, target-dependent library pass rather than the inherited target-invariant autoprefixer. Vendor declarations precede and cannot replace the authored standard, selector variants are separate rules because ordinary selector lists are unforgiving, prefixed keyframes are complete clones, and every partial, annotated, unsupported, manual, mixed, functional, descriptor, recovered, or conflicting boundary defaults to authored output. Stable target flags remain unavailable until later public option work can carry the strict query; this compatibility pass cannot hitchhike into `--optimize`.
- `TREE-001`: dead-code and critical-CSS extraction are explicit experimental subset operations over caller-declared closed selector domains, not source-only optimization guesses. Only complete class/ID inventories and direct positive requirements grant absence authority; every functional, document-language-dependent, dependency-bearing, dynamic, incomplete, or generated-overlap boundary remains authored. The recovery CLI continues to reject extraction flags.
- Milestone 3 exit: `--optimize` now reaches only the closed verified preset and never the inherited optimizer. Bounded fixed-point rounds make the composed CLI result byte-stable even when one proof-backed transformation exposes another candidate after reparse; separately authorized prefix and extraction behavior remains outside the preset.
- `API-001`: the public foundation is exercised from a separate consumer module rather than only by tests rooted inside `src/lib.zig`. The external name is `zigcss`; final high-level compile options/results and package consumption remain dependency-ordered later packages rather than accidental claims of this scaffold.
- `API-002`: the public compile facade has one explicit ownership boundary. Callers borrow input/options only for the call and receive independently owned CSS, maps, diagnostics, dependency facts, and future module-export storage; incoherent options and bounded dependency failures are structured result diagnostics, while operational failures remain errors.
- `API-003`: native plugins are an explicitly experimental trusted-code adapter over `PASS-001`, not a second mutable transform engine. Plugin registration order is irrelevant, policy and namespace boundaries are mechanical, compiler state is transactional, and callback-owned external side effects remain outside rollback.
- `BUILD-001`: the Zig package now has a stable identity and minimum compiler declaration backed by real path and fetched-cache consumers. Its initial allowlist exports only the build entry point, manifest, compiler sources, README, and license; remote publication and build helpers remain unavailable until their own gates pass.
- `BUILD-002`: the dependency build module exports a narrow supported helper rather than the inherited mutable custom step. Each invocation is a cacheable one-input/one-output graph node, downstream consumers receive the generated `LazyPath`, and unavailable CLI features cannot be smuggled through helper options.
- `BUILD-003`: every authored Zig example now has an executable build gate. The complete path-dependency project separates application and host-tool targets, consumes the public API and supported build helper, exact-checks and installs generated CSS, and passes Debug/ReleaseSafe CI.
- `CLI-010`: all supported CLI CSS modes now share the exact owned public compile contract used by Zig consumers. The executable owns only command, path, I/O, diagnostic-rendering, and write orchestration; direct parser/optimizer/emitter implementations are absent from its runtime boundary.
- `CLI-011`: the public command surface now has synchronized version/syntax metadata, bounded stdin and explicit stdout, non-repeatable options, mechanically disjoint stream/batch/watch modes, and documented 0/1/2 statuses. The npm launcher preserves the same streams, statuses, and POSIX signal semantics.
- `CLI-012`: successful file output now commits by same-directory atomic replacement with late parent creation; deterministic bounded batch names disambiguate equal stems without depending on argument order, while canonical/inode collision checks remain authoritative.
- `WATCH-001`: watch mode now compiles the root snapshot it already hashed, observes direct local relative imports from owned public dependency evidence, retains dependency coverage across failed attempts, and waits after unchanged failures instead of recompiling twice per second.
- `PARALLEL-001`: batch compilation now uses at most eight dynamically queued workers, one stable allocator lifetime per task, cancellation before further claims, unconditional joins, leak-checked unwind, and argument-ordered diagnostics and atomic commits.
- `MODULE-001`: the Zig library now exposes one closed CSS Modules slice with local-by-default typed class scoping, versioned source-specific names, owned first-seen exports, bounded metadata, strict no-partial-output failures, generated-CSS reparsing, and an independent naming/Lightning CSS oracle; CLI and LSP exposure remain contained.

- `VSCODE-001`: the editor artifact is a reproducible client package, not an implicit binary distribution channel. Its empty-path lookup cannot execute from the workspace/current directory, explicit invalid configuration fails rather than silently changing authority, runtime checks mirror manifest limits, and the temporary packaging gate admits an exact reviewed file inventory before cleanup. Publication and native-binary bundling remain unauthorized.

- `NEOVIM-001`: the plugin-framework prototype is replaced by a CSS-only built-in configuration whose minimum/current Neovim clients launch the real absolute command, prove exact advertised/absent methods and compiler-backed requests, and cleanly stop without touching user editor state.

- `DOC-001`: one evidence-linked metadata source now drives the repository README, published status guide, and site feature table; validation and CI reject unsupported status claims, missing or escaping evidence, invented commands, malformed presentation markup, and generated-table drift.

- `DOC-002`: every tracked Markdown/rendered-site code example is classified and syntax-checked, compiled, executed, or explicitly hash-pinned as a non-executable design sketch; every internal Markdown and literal site route is resolved under canonical repository confinement with fragment and reference-label validation.

- `DEP-001`: all npm manifests/lockfiles and exact direct pins are inventory-owned, every production lock graph is audited in CI with high/critical findings fatal, and bounded weekly Dependabot version PRs cover npm, GitHub Actions, and Docker without merge, credential, publish, or deploy authority.

- `REL-001`: one roadmap-bound `VERSION` authority now closes every current release identity claim, package/lock/runtime/container/formula/editor surface, generated capability row, CI preflight, and exact release tag; Marketplace's three-part version is explicitly mapped to the core prerelease and cannot package without its prerelease marker.

- `REL-002`: every release target now has a closed five-file identity—archive, manifest, SPDX SBOM, signed SLSA provenance bundle, and signed SPDX bundle—whose names, bytes, predicates, permissions, action pins, verification order, and upload scope fail closed before release creation. Local structural bundle tests do not claim cryptographic proof; the tag workflow must obtain GitHub OIDC and pass `gh attestation verify` before its artifacts can move downstream.

- `REL-003`: npm, Docker, and Homebrew now consume immutable, checksum-bound inputs under closed target and inventory contracts. The npm installer and Docker image consume the signed-release asset names; the unpublished Homebrew source formula pins an existing commit archive, its verified digest, and Zig 0.15, with exact live compiler output and no mutable head or second-download path.

- `REL-004`: every workflow defaults to no token authority, each job receives its exact minimum grant, and every external action is a full reviewed commit pin guarded by a closed dependency-free policy before npm installation.

- `REL-005`: every release archive is executed on a matching architecture before attestation, and the same checksum-bound bytes must survive an offline real npm pack/postinstall/wrapper lifecycle without network fallback or residue before publication can become reachable.

- `BENCH-001`: benchmark inputs now come from one generated versioned inventory whose exact bytes, hashes, rule/declaration complexity, file types, and active Zig embedding are checked in CI; changing corpus identity is an explicit reviewed input change rather than runtime generation.
- `BENCH-002`: competitor identity now comes from one closed manifest plus exact npm package/lock records. Only the checksum-locked native optional package selected by `npm ci --ignore-scripts` may execute, and it is invoked by an absolute repository-confined path; the historical networked runner and its unverifiable timing archives are gone.
- `BENCH-003`: output acceptance is a mandatory fail-closed boundary, not a post-processing check. Every tool/corpus emission must be nonempty, bounded, recovery-free, warning-free at the oracle, and semantically canonical to the exact input before a positive timing duration may enter a sample collection. The current Zig smoke adds an independent typed-AST structural check and deliberately records no duration; execution-mode and measurement design remain `BENCH-004`.
- `BENCH-004`: cross-tool comparison is confined to identical new-process CLI boundaries, with startup included in both cold/no-local-warmup and warm/one-validated-local-warmup modes. ZigCSS direct API latency, allocator-requested memory, and in-process throughput are separate non-comparative metrics; every timed output is validated before an in-memory admission, and no raw value survives the smoke.
- `BENCH-005`: every report series now retains exactly 20 raw observations and derives median, nearest-rank p95, and population variance from those exact values. The complete report is valid only when all 43 ordered CLI/API/memory/throughput series, corpus and executable digests, and closed host/toolchain metadata agree; collection or validation failure produces no report. CI proves the contract with a temporary ReleaseFast report and deletes it, leaving controlled scheduling and archival to `BENCH-006` and public rendering to `BENCH-007`.
- `BENCH-006`: benchmark collection is schedule-only on one dedicated self-hosted Linux x64 label and one non-cancelling concurrency group. Each accepted archive contains exactly the complete validated report and a provenance manifest binding source/run identity, report bytes, and a stable hardware fingerprint; persistent-runner cleanup is unconditional. GitHub artifact retention is the maximum reviewed 90-day handoff window, not publication or a permanent performance claim. `BENCH-007` may render only a separately obtained archive that passes this verifier.
- `BENCH-007` implementation: public Markdown is now a deterministic view of one committed verified archive rather than editable timing prose. Until that exact controlled input exists, the selected state is a byte-exact withdrawal with no sample, statistic, comparison, ranking, or ratio. Synthetic fixture archives prove the renderer and rejection boundaries but cannot authorize a public result.

## Active blockers
