Direct non-force integration is green through code/ledger head `c34deaf`: Build run `29251213253` completed the full Test Suite and all five native compiler/archive/npm matrix jobs successfully. No actionable repository or integration fault remains ahead of controlled archive collection.
- The GitHub repository Actions runner API returned `total_count: 0`. Therefore no runner currently satisfies the required `[self-hosted, linux, x64, zigcss-benchmark-v1]` label set. Registering and keeping a dedicated non-emulated Linux x64 runner online requires operator-owned hardware and GitHub registration authority; substituting GitHub-hosted or emulated hardware would invalidate `BENCH-006` evidence and is not an acceptable workaround. The 2026-07-13 04:17 UTC schedule has passed; after green replacement CI, the next policy-conforming scheduled opportunity is 2026-07-20 04:17 UTC. No qualifying archive currently exists.

## Known regressions and risks

The authoritative regression list remains the Milestone 0 list in `DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md`. Base-commit reproductions established:

- The previously recorded nested callable-producing module call inside an otherwise unknown CSS function is closed by `NSASS-011-PLAIN-CSS-FUNCTION-ARGUMENTS`: arguments now evaluate before serialization and non-CSS callable values fail closed. The private native path remains production-unreachable and publicly unclaimed; lazy conditional-function semantics remain an explicit future boundary.

| Area | Base-commit evidence |
|---|---|
| Static traversal | `GET /..%2fpackage.json` served `docs/package.json`; double traversal served repository `package.json`. |
| Malformed URL | `GET /%E0%A4%A` threw `URIError: URI malformed`, terminated the docs server, and returned an empty reply. |
| Compile-service limits | Code inspection found unbounded request accumulation, no timeout/output/concurrency/process limit, synchronous temp I/O, and incomplete error-path cleanup. |
| Compound selectors | `.a.b` and `.a .b` both emitted `.a .b`, proving semantic collapse. |
| Functional/attribute selectors and nested delimiters | A selector containing `:not(...)` and `[data-x=...]` failed at byte 1 instead of parsing. |
| Declaration-bearing at-rules | `@font-face` failed as an invalid identifier. |
| Percentage keyframes and at-rule spacing | The combined at-rule corpus could not parse; optimized media output elsewhere emitted invalid `@mediascreen`. |
| Important/fallback ordering | Three `color` declarations collapsed to only the last important value. |
| Rule/at-rule ordering | Optimization merged non-adjacent `.keep` rules and non-adjacent `@media` blocks, moved the media block, reversed nested rule order, and reported allocator leaks. |
| Custom-property cascade | Optimization replaced `var(--theme)` with a global/static value and shortened custom property values, ignoring dynamic cascade semantics. |
| Logical/reset behavior | Optimization converted `margin-inline-start` to `margin-left` despite RTL/vertical modes and synthesized a background shorthand. |
| Source-map/browser flags | `--source-map` and `--browsers chrome100` were accepted but output was unchanged and no source map was produced. |
| Unknown/missing CLI arguments | An unknown flag and a missing `-o` value both exited 0 and compiled normally. |
| Profiling lifecycle | `--profile` printed a report and then crashed with SIGABRT/segmentation fault (exit 134), confirming the double-end path. |
| Input overwrite | `input.css -o input.css --minify` exited 0 and replaced the input in place. |
| Batch collision | Two different `shared.css` inputs mapped to the same output, both reported success, and the second silently overwrote the first. |

## Decisions

- The approved plan is authoritative.
- Security and semantic containment precede feature work.
- Inherited work is preserved and will not be rewritten or discarded.
- `NATIVE-004` treats each canonical root as an already-open filesystem capability; a candidate URL names a path but grants no authority. Traversal is relative to that capability with no-follow opens, while canonical path rebinding before and after the read proves the returned URL still names the same regular-file identity. Every load request, including malformed or missing probes, consumes the attempt ceiling. Nonempty file-URL authorities and Windows network-share roots remain fail-closed; language-specific partial, extension, package, glob, and load-path search belongs to later native adapters and cannot widen this shared loader.
- `NATIVE-005` makes language evaluation a private transaction rather than an output buffer. Generated bytes become a result only after the complete stylesheet passes the existing core with no error/recovery diagnostic; every evaluator, cancellation, limit, allocation, or validation failure closes the resolver and exposes no CSS. Language-specific candidate search remains outside the transaction, while successful results deep-own the resolver facts needed by later watch and product integration. Phase-boundary cancellation is cooperative; the core remains the sole CSS grammar authority.
- `OPS-002` replaces unreliable unattended continuity with one serialized outer Bash loop, not concurrent agents. Exactly one continuation owner may touch this worktree at a time; the background runner uses fresh ephemeral context from the approved roadmap and durable ledger, while local ignored state owns only scheduling evidence. System-startup installation remains outside authority, so `start` lasts for the current login and must be reissued after logout/reboot. A dirty restart is admitted only when its exact fingerprint was recorded by an interrupted runner pass.
- `OPS-003` trusts result markers only from Codex's isolated final-message file, never from the mixed diagnostic transcript. On macOS the current app-bundled CLI precedes an optional user-local installation because the selected model/runtime compatibility contract is stricter than path preference; model failure remains an error and never authorizes fallback.
- `OPS-004` makes automatic pushing a supervisor privilege, not a model capability. Only a clean green `HEAD` may move the identically named current `vale/*` branch on the closed approved `origin`; ordinary push plus exact readback is required, force and alternate refspecs/remotes remain absent, and any failure stops locally.
- `OPS-005` uses a detached system `screen` session for per-login durability because it retains the caller's authorized access to linked-worktree Git metadata. A standalone launchd process does not inherit that macOS privacy authority and is therefore not a valid runner transport. No LaunchAgent, login item, or system-startup state is installed.
- `OPS-006` treats blocker identity as a protocol value rather than human prose. One lowercase kebab-case code names the underlying authority or external-state condition across passes; the reason remains readable and may change. Invalid identity pauses fail closed, the same code counts toward the existing three-pass bound, and a different code alone starts a new blocker count.
- `OPS-007` separates production branch authority from hermetic runner testing. Only explicit test mode may bypass the `vale/*` requirement; production controls never set that mode, so a normal `main` checkout cannot start the autonomous loop.
- GitHub expression contexts are resolved at their narrowest valid scope. `runner.temp` belongs only to steps after runner assignment, never job-level `env`, and the archive policy owns all four uses.
- The reviewed Zig 0.15.2 platform baseline uses explicit `macos-15` and `macos-15-intel` runners. Floating `macos-latest` is not an acceptable release identity because its SDK can change independently of the compiler baseline.
- CLI integration children use absolute, repository-confined temporary-directory paths through `Child.cwd`; Zig 0.15.2's directory-handle `cwd_dir` remains unavailable on Windows. Release-consumer CI fetches full history only where the immutable Homebrew source ancestry proof requires it.
- The compile API will be disabled after `SEC-001` unless `SEC-002` isolation is implemented and verified immediately.
- Legacy compiler failures are executable characterization quarantines, not accepted contracts. Each owning fix must replace its quarantine with the correct behavior assertion in the same commit.
- Legacy optimizer functions remain reachable only to explicitly labeled internal tests. They are not part of the stable CLI/code-generation path and must not be re-enabled without pass-specific acceptance evidence.
- Operator-requested post-Milestone-0 tooling: add a ZigCSS autonomous-loop protocol plus a read-only `orient.sh`, modeled on Alvo's protocol/orient split and adapted to this repository's no-push/no-deploy authority boundary.
- Alternate-format parsers remain available only as experimental internals for characterization; `.scss`, `.sass`, `.less`, `.styl`, `.postcss`, `.module.css`, and CSS-in-JS extensions are rejected by the recovery CLI before any output is written.
- The persistent Codex goal remains the scheduler. `scripts/autodevelop/orient.sh` is deliberately read-only and cannot launch a model, wake a task, or mutate repository/external state; `docs/operations/codex-loop-protocol.md` is the canonical resume procedure.
- Foundational implementation must conform to accepted `ADR-001`, `ADR-002`, `ADR-003`, `ADR-004`, and `ADR-010`; changing stable scope, source/syntax boundaries, ownership, transform authority, or autonomous runtime requires a superseding approved ADR.
- CSS strings use their distinct trailing-backslash-at-EOF rule when producing decoded values; identifiers, dimensions, hashes, at-keywords, and URLs retain the general escaped-code-point replacement behavior.
- The arena control block is separately allocated from its caller-provided backing allocator so allocators retained by movable child containers never point into a relocated `Compilation` value.
- Component-value recursion defaults to a 256-container nesting budget and returns `NestingLimitExceeded` before exceeding it; resource-limit diagnostics are added by `DIAG-001` rather than conflated with allocation failures.
- The allocation-free tokenizer records bounded recovery metadata on tokens; the compilation-backed syntax layer converts it to owned diagnostics so no allocation error can be swallowed inside `Tokenizer.next`.
- The new `src/css/ast.zig` model coexists with the inherited `src/ast.zig` prototype until Milestone 2 parsing/emission migration; stable recovery paths do not consume the legacy selector representation.
- Declaration lists are ordered slices rather than property maps, preserving fallback declarations and leaving cascade/importance decisions to later explicit passes.
- Rule lists remain ordered and recursive through pointers, preventing non-adjacent at-rules/rules from being merged by the data model itself.
- Column combinators remain representable in the AST but are not accepted by the stable parser because the current Selectors Level 4 grammar lists only descendant, child, next-sibling, and subsequent-sibling combinators. Native nesting accepts explicit `&`, leading combinators, and implicit parent references; a recursively encountered ampersand delimiter suppresses implicit-parent insertion metadata.
- Parsed declarations retain the complete raw value (including importance trivia/marker) while `valueWithoutImportance()` exposes the semantic prefix; no property map or early value interpretation may remove fallback order.
- Generic rule-list at-rules (`media`, `supports`, `container`, `layer`, `scope`, and related forms) recurse immediately; keyframes retain raw frame syntax for PAR-004, and page/unknown mixed grammars stay raw until their dedicated classifier can preserve all nested forms.
- Structured at-rule specialization keeps generic blocks as the recovery boundary. Keyframes retain the full raw frame stream alongside typed valid frames; page remains a raw mixed-content block alongside ordered typed declarations/margin boxes; malformed typed preludes never erase the underlying rule.
- Rule recovery uses the shared `css/recovery.zig` boundary contract: semicolon and complete curly-block boundaries always advance one component, EOF is stable, nested components remain opaque, and syntax diagnostics remain the sole report for stray closing tokens.
- Pretty emission follows CSSOM identifier/string escaping and CSS Syntax round-trip constraints. Comments inside raw component values are retained as token separators; generic structural whitespace is canonicalized; incomplete or partially lowered constructs are rejected before a successful output is returned.
- Pretty and minified emission are modes of the same implementation. Minified mode defaults to no final newline, may omit only a declaration immediately before its closing brace, and retains a semicolon when a following page item still needs an explicit declaration boundary.
- Unknown at-rule preludes and raw blocks preserve adjacency and edge whitespace-token presence in both emission modes. Pretty formatting may not insert a cosmetic separator into untyped syntax, and minification may collapse raw whitespace width but not erase the token.
- CSS source-map columns are zero-based UTF-16 code-unit offsets per ECMA-426. The map JSON includes `version: 3`, one ordered source, optional `sourcesContent`/generated file, no names, and strict four-field mappings; generated output and source-map ownership remain separate.
- Style blocks partition their source span between leading declarations and ordered child rules. Invalid block-bearing child rules still split declaration runs as CSS Syntax requires; their source gap remains explicit so both emitter modes reject recovered output rather than silently deleting authored bytes.
- Known declaration/keyframe/page/top-level-only at-rules are invalid in a nesting context and recover at their complete at-rule boundary. Raw unknown at-rules remain represented for forward compatibility; newly standardized forms require an explicit classifier/context decision.
- Stable parse diagnostics are emitted only after the complete typed parse and successful compilation produces no partial CSS. Parallel batch compilation likewise writes no destination until every task has compiled successfully.
- Independent grammar validation is pinned to exact Lightning CSS package bytes through `package-lock.json`. An upgrade changes the evidence baseline and must update the declared version and rerun both output modes for every fixture.
- Transform safety classes are independent permissions, not an ordinal hierarchy. Policy defaults to verified analysis only; transitive dependencies need the same explicit authority as requested passes. Stable `--optimize` is now confined to the static seven-definition preset whose complete membership has passed the acceptance suite.
- Transform deletions are proof-carrying AST entries, not trusted source spans. The emitter independently validates that an omitted rule is structurally empty and belongs to the closed cleanup set before skipping it; recursive omission proofs retain nested source coverage and surviving source maps never point into deleted rules.
- `zigcss-transform-test-driver` is installed only by `zig build test` for independent pass validation and does not enter the normal install artifact. Its individual compatibility/extraction routes remain test-only; its `verified-optimizer` route mirrors the stable CLI preset for independent differential validation.
- Duplicate analysis uses deterministic first-occurrence output order and bounded hash-backed counting, but copies report names and spans into caller-owned storage so results can safely outlive compilation. Default group, occurrence, property-byte, and recursion limits prevent report construction from becoming an unbounded resource sink.
- Numeric grammar and unit behavior are frozen by fixtures against the reviewed CSS Values and Units Level 4 and CSS Conditional Rules Level 5 drafts; dimension/percentage algebra follows CSS Typed OM Level 1. Living-spec changes require an intentional fixture and decision update rather than silently changing an optimizer result.
- `VAL-001` stores postfix typing evidence only. Context-dependent conversion, finite-value handling, precision policy, folding, and shortening remain `MATH-001` or later semantic-rewrite responsibilities and default to no rewrite when evidence is incomplete.
- Generated nodes use the smallest complete authored construct as their causal source span. Emission adds one mapping at the generated segment start and never invents inner mappings, borrows an adjacent span, or defaults to source offset zero.
- `MATH-001` folds only complete supported math-function values under an exact property category/range policy. Identical-unit finite arithmetic is eligible; cross-unit conversion, comments, substitutions, descriptors, unsupported properties, invalid ranges, ambiguous signed zero, and non-shorter output retain the exact input root.
- `OPT-012` treats compatibility as part of equivalence: opaque basic named/hex/RGB forms share the oldest closed output surface, while non-opaque functional/keyword colors remain authored unless alpha-hex support was already required. Unitless-zero shortening excludes percentage/dimension categories and every number-or-length ambiguity.
- `OPT-013` treats shorthand expansion as a reset-and-cascade proof, not a textual compression. Only an adjacent top/right/bottom/left group with identical importance and raw value spelling may become one `margin` declaration; logical properties, fallbacks, layers, media queries, and nested segments remain on the same side of the replacement boundary. Page and page-margin descriptor contexts are excluded until an independent computed/canonical oracle can verify them.
- `PREFIX-002` treats compatibility syntax as structured proof output. Complete unannotated forms may be added in deterministic target order, but the standard form remains last and no qualifier, unsupported gap, author-supplied vendor policy, or untyped selector context is interpreted into authority. Stable CLI exposure is a separate public API decision, not an automatic consequence of pass verification.
- `TREE-001` treats selector extraction as a closed-snapshot proof. Null inventory categories never mean empty, critical input must include the full selector-matching render tree, ASCII case variants conservatively retain rules, and one possible selector alternative preserves the entire style rule. The two modes remain experimental despite full package evidence and require two independent policy grants.
- Milestone 3 stable optimization is a closed preset, not a maturity wildcard. Because proof-carrying AST nodes deliberately block overlapping rewrites inside one source snapshot, the CLI reaches a bounded emitted-byte fixed point through validated reparses. Source maps are not exposed across those intermediate snapshots until a composed-map proof exists.
- `API-001` reuses the ownership-safe library root established by `ARCH-001` instead of creating a parallel facade. External-consumer compilation is the export gate; a symbol used only through implementation-relative imports does not count as public. The existing low-level modules remain experimental until `API-002` defines the stable compile facade.
- `API-002` keeps target queries caller-owned and borrowed only for the duration of `compile`; no target-query pointer enters a result. Dependencies are facts, not fetch instructions: only decoded top-level string/URL `@import` operands are reported, authored order and duplicates remain observable, unsupported preludes are not guessed, and resource-limit failure discards partial facts. Fixed-point optimization remains incompatible with source maps until intermediate mappings can be composed. Stable CSS deliberately returns `module_exports = null` pending `MODULE-001`.
- `API-003` confines native plugins to `plugin.` IDs and borrowed synchronous lifetimes. The closed optimizer finishes before the plugin plan; target prefixing participates in that final plan under global phase/priority/ID order. Plugin-only policy validation happens before compatibility authority is added for the built-in prefix pass, so no plugin can gain a class transitively. Active plugin source maps remain unavailable, `API0002` identifies invalid configuration, `API0003` identifies transactional execution failure, and native panic/UB/nontermination requires consumer process isolation rather than a false in-process recovery promise.
- `BUILD-001` assigns package identity fingerprint `0xae272a4871e93d07`; changing it would change package identity and requires an explicit ownership decision, not version maintenance. Version 0.3.0 matches the current npm manifest, and `minimum_zig_version = "0.15.2"` names the earliest tested baseline while CI remains pinned to that exact toolchain. The initial `.paths` list deliberately excludes `build_helpers.zig` until `BUILD-002`, and no remote URL/hash is documented before a published artifact exists.
- `BUILD-002` treats CSS compilation as a normal `Step.Run` assembled before execution: `LazyPath` input, generated output argument, tool dependency, bounded stdio, and exact downstream check. Multiple files require multiple independent helper calls, which preserves per-file cache keys and declared outputs. Portable output basenames prevent caller-selected paths from escaping the build cache. The artifact is a host tool; application cross targets must not be executed as build tools. Unsupported CLI features are absent rather than represented as accepted no-ops.
- `BUILD-003` treats examples as executable consumer contracts, not illustrative snippets. The build project obtains a separate host dependency for the compiler tool and a target dependency for application imports; CI compiles both supported safe modes, while a repository enumeration test fails when an authored Zig example has no declared build path. Generated caches and install outputs are never considered authored examples.
- `CLI-010` defines the executable/library boundary at `zigcss.compile`: option selection and owned-result consumption belong to the CLI, while parsing, transforms, dependencies, and emission stay in the library. The separately experimental LSP route retains its explicitly documented legacy boundary until Milestone 6. Because the public facade has no truthful internal timing hooks, `--profile` reports one end-to-end compile duration instead of inventing stage precision; `PROF-010` owns accurate stage and allocator metrics.
- `CLI-011` reserves `-` exclusively as the standard stream sentinel. Stdin is bounded by the same 10 MiB CLI input limit, receives the stable diagnostic name `<stdin>`, and is deliberately single-shot rather than watch/batch input. Only CSS syntax is accepted; future syntax tags require their owning format packages. Exit 0 means success/info, 1 means compilation or operational I/O failure, and 2 means caller usage/configuration failure. Informational/CSS bytes use stdout while warnings/errors use stderr. A POSIX signal remains a signal through the npm launcher rather than being flattened into an invented numeric success.
- `CLI-012` defines atomicity at one destination, using a temporary sibling and rename rather than in-place truncation. Parent creation is deliberately delayed until a successful result is committed, but a batch does not promise rollback of earlier independently committed files after a later operational failure. Existing destination link entries are replaced rather than followed. Batch names derive only from the final stem and, when needed, a normalized relative-path hash; order never participates, basename length is bounded, and the final path-identity gate remains the collision authority.
- `WATCH-001` treats the root's already-read bytes as the one compile snapshot and records that snapshot before attempting compilation. Error state is a watch state rather than a retry request. Direct dependency facts are watched conservatively only when they are local relative filesystem paths; URL schemes and absolute/origin-relative forms are not reinterpreted into local access, queries/fragments are not path bytes, imports are not recursively parsed or inlined, and failed roots retain the last valid direct set. Path deduplication follows the same platform case policy as output planning, while content and read-error transitions remain deterministic.
- `PARALLEL-001` bounds concurrency at eight even on larger hosts and schedules through one locked monotonic index rather than static chunks. Cancellation is cooperative at the public compile-call boundary: already-running tasks finish and are cleaned, but no new task is opened after the queue observes failure. Per-task GPAs deliberately disable their own mutex because ownership never crosses workers; task slots are fully planned before any allocator is first used and remain stable through main-thread deinit. Thread-creation failure is a cancellation path, not an unwind past live handles. Completion order never grants write or diagnostic order; the input plan is the sole commit order.
- `PROF-010` defines profile memory as allocator-requested ownership evidence, not RSS, heap capacity, or a platform memory claim. One public compile session owns the timer and stage boundaries; the CLI renders its returned metrics instead of maintaining a second clock. The forwarding allocator adds no headers and returns the caller allocator's exact pointers, allowing result-owned allocations to outlive the stack wrapper; its final live-byte snapshot is therefore the retained result size before caller cleanup. Skipped and sub-timer-resolution stages may be zero, while unavailable timing support is an explicit operational failure rather than fabricated data.
- `FMT-001` treats every inherited alternate-format implementation as characterized legacy code, not a positive compatibility claim. Availability, evidence, strategy, sources, and owning packages live in one executable matrix. Real extension probes and the CSS-only public enum are the current product contract. ADR-005 chooses removal for every heuristic ecosystem adapter except a future closed CSS Modules subset; ADR-011 now owns the unrelated native-plugin decision without an identifier collision.
- `SCSS-001` makes ADR-005 removal physical: neither Sass syntax has a parser source, legacy dispatch tag, or CSS fallback. The stable CLI remains the extension-facing rejection authority; the experimental LSP diagnoses unsupported/removed URIs. Activating its previously dormant test artifact also fixes URI ownership so one allocation is never freed as both map key and value. With no chosen native scope, `SCSS-002` is not applicable unless a new decision funds canonical integration.
- `LESS-001` removes the global at-variable substitution path rather than treating a handful of examples as a Less grammar. The source, tag, and dispatch are absent; `.less` remains a real CLI rejection and a null detector/LSP diagnostic path. Any future Less work requires a new funded scope or canonical-integration decision.
- `STYLUS-001` removes line/indent guessing and swallowed variable errors rather than advertising a Stylus subset. The source, tag, and dispatch are absent; `.styl` remains an explicit CLI and LSP rejection. Future Stylus work requires a new funded language/integration decision.
- `MODULE-001` treats the compile-call source name as a required module identity, normalizes only path separators, and hashes length-delimited identity plus decoded class bytes under the versioned `zigcss.css-modules.v1` domain. Full SHA-256 output avoids order-dependent suffixes; a distinct-name digest collision is an internal failure, not permission to weaken determinism. Export collection owns first occurrence order while emission uses a separate byte-sorted lookup. The initial grammar is local-by-default and deny-by-default: unsupported module constructs or selector-bearing raw syntax produce `CSS0009` with no CSS, and optimizer/prefix/plugin composition awaits its own semantic evidence.
- `MODULE-002` scope uses only canonical functional `:local(...)` and `:global(...)` wrappers. Bare scope-state switching is intentionally rejected as ambiguous. Class rewrites are keyed by exact source occurrence rather than decoded name, wrapper fragments are parsed silently into the compilation arena, and the emitter removes only adapter-validated wrapper spans. The initial class-only contract rejects IDs rather than preserving them under a false locality claim.
- `MODULE-002` composition mirrors the useful unresolved-reference shape of the pinned Lightning CSS oracle rather than flattening external module state the compiler does not own. Local references point to owned generated names, global references retain decoded identifiers, and dependency references retain decoded export/specifier pairs. One external declaration yields one bounded `.css_module` dependency fact; ZigCSS never opens, resolves, or executes a dependency. Plain-class targets and acyclic local graphs are stricter than permissive ecosystem behavior by design.
- `MODULE-002` local values are a deliberately closed single-file substitution pass. Definitions resolve only earlier definitions so their serialized exports are deterministic; ordinary CSS is scanned after collection so authored CSS may use a later definition. Exact tokenizer identity, not substring matching, controls replacement. Strings and URLs are never opened, and imported values/raw ICSS remain strict errors because this compiler call owns neither a resolver nor external module state. Class and value exports intentionally share one namespace and first-authored order.
- `JS-001` makes ADR-005 removal physical: the repository contains no JavaScript/TypeScript byte scanner, expression-deletion path, legacy tag, or parser dispatch. `.css.js` and `.css.ts` stay recognizable only at the stable CLI rejection boundary and deliberately become null in the legacy detector so the LSP cannot reinterpret application code as CSS. Any future extraction requires a funded, version-pinned JavaScript/TypeScript parser and a non-executing static contract for tagged templates and dynamic expressions.
- `POSTCSS-001` removes the directive scanner rather than laundering fixed deletion/expansion behavior under the PostCSS name. `.postcss` remains only an explicit CLI rejection and null legacy-detector result; there is no plugin host, tag, or dispatch. Removing the scanner also makes the separate fixed utility registry unreachable, but that source remains characterized until `TAILWIND-001` owns its deletion and evidence.
- `TAILWIND-001` removes the unreachable fixed utility registry rather than preserving the Tailwind name for a finite table that omitted unknown utilities. Compiler-side removal does not alter the documentation site's separate pinned canonical Tailwind build dependency. Any future ZigCSS utility preset needs its own non-Tailwind name and exact semantics; claiming Tailwind requires canonical, versioned compilation evidence.
- `LSP-001` treats framing as a bounded owned byte transport, not JSON or method semantics. Exact CRLF header termination, one decimal `Content-Length`, 8 KiB aggregate headers, 16 MiB bodies, sequential frames, and clean boundary EOF are the complete admitted scope. Malformed transport input remains an operational error until `LSP-002` defines structured protocol errors; method and document lifecycle belongs to `LSP-003`.
- `LSP-002` admits only a JSON-RPC 2.0 object envelope with a string method and a string, number, or null ID. Standard parsing and serialization own all escaping and number spelling; parse/envelope/method/parameter faults are protocol results rather than process crashes. Handler validation occurs before response mutation, while committed document/root state remains owned even if later serialization fails. Notifications intentionally still receive the inherited response until `LSP-003` introduces a request/notification result type and lifecycle state machine.
- `LSP-003` uses an explicit synchronous lifecycle rather than pretending cancellation concurrency. A valid notification never owns response bytes. Initialization, running, and shutdown are disjoint states; only an exit notification produces process control, and EOF at a frame boundary remains a clean transport completion. Full document synchronization is the sole advertised capability: client-owned open text, strictly increasing full replacements, and balanced close ownership. Legacy diagnostic/navigation handlers remain unadvertised and are scheduled for replacement by `LSP-004` through `LSP-006`.
- `LSP-004` fixes the server's protocol coordinate system at UTF-16, which every LSP client and server must support and which remains the default when no alternate encoding is negotiated. One converter owns both directions between client positions and source bytes, with strict scalar/CRLF boundaries and specified line-end clamping. Existing editor handlers use it for positional integrity but remain unadvertised until compiler diagnostics and syntax/workspace indexes replace their legacy semantics in `LSP-005` and `LSP-006`.
- `LSP-005` uses document diagnostic pulls rather than inventing a push scheduler in the synchronous server. It advertises no inter-file/workspace claim, emits no result ID, and returns a complete current report for the client-owned document version on every standard singular request. Compiler diagnostics are consumed only through the external public module; unsupported document kinds remain a separate containment diagnostic. No response bytes are begun until parameters, compilation, and every UTF-16 range have been validated.
- `LSP-006` treats a workspace as deterministic synchronized state, not permission to crawl the filesystem. One typed index is lazily owned by each open CSS document version; full replacement and close are its only invalidation boundaries. Class and ID selectors refer to external DOM symbols and therefore have no fabricated stylesheet definition, while custom properties and keyframe names have grammar-owned definition/reference relationships. Ambiguous shorthand/value syntax and opaque content default to no result. All workspace state, index allocation, search work, result counts, rename input, and escaped response bytes have explicit fail-closed budgets, and an over-budget build or update leaves the previous owned state intact.
- `LSP-007` closes the server package with composition evidence rather than another implementation fork. The final child transcript combines malformed-request recovery, a genuinely large synchronized document, Unicode/CRLF coordinate conversion, compiler diagnostics, index navigation, notification silence, close invalidation, shutdown/exit, and exact frame consumption. Repeated allocator-checked index lifecycles prove persistent counters and ownership return to zero; component-level exhaustive allocation tests remain the stronger proof for every injected failure point. Editor distribution, discovery, and client integration remain separately owned by `VSCODE-001` and `NEOVIM-001`.

- `VSCODE-001` treats the extension as a reproducible client artifact, not implicit binary distribution authority. Discovery never executes a relative/workspace path, an invalid explicit setting fails without fallback, runtime validation mirrors schema limits, and package verification admits an exact reviewed inventory before deleting generated output. Publishing and native-binary bundling remain separate unauthorized decisions.

- `NEOVIM-001` treats editor configuration as process-launch authority. Explicit paths are absolute verified executables and never fall back; automatic lookup ignores relative/empty `PATH` entries and is bounded on both directories and Windows extensions. The workspace root controls client grouping only, not file discovery. Configuration publishes no mapping for an unadvertised method, and pinned real clients—not Lua text alone—are the graduation gate.

- `DOC-001` treats public capability status as generated test evidence rather than prose copied across surfaces. Metadata may cite only a closed status kind and executable gate with repository-confined content anchors; Markdown tables are generated views, and the site consumes the same rows directly. Unknown, contradictory, stale, or unanchored claims are build failures.

- `DOC-002` treats code formatting as an executable claim and links as repository-bound references. Canonical Zig examples are exact source files that compile and run in the main safe-mode graph; CSS output must also parse independently; shell, JSON, Lua, and Vim snippets use their real syntax parsers without executing publication instructions or loading user editor state. New languages, dynamic rendered examples, missing labels/routes/fragments, and paths that escape lexically or through symlinks fail closed.

- `DEP-001` treats dependency state as the union of an exact manifest/lock inventory, live production advisory evidence, and review-only update proposals. Direct versions stay exact, new lock roots require an explicit policy update, audit transport/schema errors cannot masquerade as clean results, and automation may open bounded pull requests but never gains registry credentials, target override, merge, publication, or deployment authority.

- `REL-001` treats `VERSION` and Milestone 9's approved `0.5.0-rc.1` candidate as one identity contract. Core npm, Zig, CLI, Docker, formula, docs, and changelog surfaces use the full SemVer; VS Marketplace uses base version 0.5.0 only because its manifest rejects prerelease identifiers and therefore must package with `--pre-release`. Formula checksum completion remains dependency-ordered under `REL-003`, and no synchronized version authorizes publication.

- `REL-002` follows the official [GitHub artifact-attestation workflow](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/how-tos/secure-your-work/use-artifact-attestations/use-artifact-attestations) and immutable [`actions/attest` v4.1.1](https://github.com/actions/attest/releases/tag/v4.1.1): the release build receives only read contents plus the required attestation/OIDC grants, while release creation retains the sole repository-content write grant. The SPDX document follows the fixed [SPDX 2.3 package fields and verification-code algorithm](https://spdx.github.io/spdx-spec/v2.3/package-information/) and describes only the binary actually shipped. Signature verification stays two-layered: bounded local subject/predicate binding plus GitHub CLI cryptographic verification; only the latter establishes trust. Actual signing is deliberately deferred to the authorized tag workflow, not simulated or claimed by local tests.

- `REL-003` treats release filenames, target headers, generated checksums, and consumer-specific trust roots as closed contracts. HTTPS alone never authorizes installation: npm and Docker must validate their exact archive/manifest/inventory and executable target before late installation, while Homebrew must bind one immutable source commit, its downloaded SHA-256, the reviewed Zig 0.15 toolchain, and exact compiler behavior. The Docker image remains a verified local artifact and the Homebrew formula remains an unpublished local source-build path; neither is a publication claim.

- `REL-004` treats a workflow action update as a reviewed supply-chain change, not a tag-following convenience. The full SHA, version comment, approved action identity, job placement, and exact permission map must advance together; a Dependabot proposal remains red until that policy evidence is reviewed. OIDC is granted only to the Pages deployment and release-attestation jobs, repository write belongs only to release creation, and all other unspecified token permissions are mechanically `none`.

- `REL-005` treats header inspection as necessary but insufficient release evidence. Each target must run on a host whose Node platform/architecture exactly matches the artifact, execute the extracted archive, and then prove the packaged npm postinstall plus wrapper using only repository-local checksum-bound assets. The smoke preload is test-only, excluded from the npm package, admits exactly the expected archive and manifest URLs, and blocks every other HTTPS request. A failed target prevents attestation/upload and therefore prevents release creation and npm publication.

- `BENCH-001` treats corpus identity as generated source evidence. Version `v1` is a closed regular-file inventory with exact bytes, lowercase SHA-256, and mechanically derived rule complexity; the active Zig smoke embeds those files and cannot substitute approximate or runtime-generated inputs. The historical competitor script and archived result JSON are not evidence. Binary pinning, output validation, mode comparability, raw samples, statistical reporting, controlled execution, and public claims remain separate dependency-ordered packages.

- `BENCH-002` treats competitor selection as a supply-chain contract, not permission to discover tools at runtime. Exact direct and platform package versions, licenses, registry archives, and SHA-512 integrity live in the npm lock; lifecycle scripts remain disabled, and only the one host-matched optional native executable may run by absolute path. Lightning CSS's mismatched internal version string is preserved as observed evidence. Tool presence and identity do not make output or timings valid; `BENCH-003` must reject semantically invalid output before any sample can enter later mode/statistics work.
- `BENCH-003` treats a successful process exit as insufficient benchmark evidence. Exact file output from each closed invocation is bounded and reparsed with recovery disabled, then compared to the canonical semantics of the exact versioned corpus. A duration is appended only after validation succeeds, so empty, malformed, warning-bearing, truncated, changed, or extra output remains a hard failure rather than a missing sample. The active smoke uses only the rebuilt public compiler and typed equivalence; the inherited benchmark timing API is removed. No current gate claims comparable modes or publishes measurements before `BENCH-004` through `BENCH-007`.
- `BENCH-004` defines cold CLI as a fresh process with startup included and zero mode-local warmups, and warm CLI as the identical command after one separately validated mode-local invocation; neither name claims that the operating system page cache was purged. Those are the only cross-tool comparison modes. ZigCSS direct API latency and throughput exclude process startup, while memory reports only allocator-requested ownership fields and admits no timing metric. Esbuild's serviced JavaScript API is not mislabeled as in-process. At that checkpoint all measured CSS remained unobservable until post-timing validation succeeded, and raw values remained unavailable pending `BENCH-005`.
- `BENCH-005` defines a complete report as 43 ordered series with 20 retained unsigned-decimal observations each. Median uses the sorted middle average, p95 uses nearest rank, and variance is the exact population value; all three are recomputed during validation so copied prose or altered summaries cannot become evidence. Reports bind to the exact corpus manifest, three CLI executables, private in-process runner, and closed host/toolchain environment. The local output path is create-only, CI keeps the report temporary, and no statistic is a public comparison until `BENCH-006` controls and archives execution and `BENCH-007` renders only those archives.
- `BENCH-006` defines controlled execution as the exact upstream main branch running on one dedicated self-hosted Linux x64 label under a serialized weekly schedule. The archive is an exact report/manifest pair whose source, run, byte digest, and hardware fingerprint must revalidate before consumption. A 90-day GitHub Actions artifact is a review handoff, not publication; `BENCH-007` must reject unverified, expired/unavailable, differently fingerprinted, or manually invented data rather than filling a report gap.
- `BENCH-007` defines publication as an exact generated view over a permanently committed copy of one retained controlled archive. The GitHub run/artifact link must agree with archive provenance, but raw samples remain available through the committed report after artifact expiry. Cross-tool tables contain only identical cold/warm CLI boundaries; ZigCSS direct API, allocator-requested memory, and throughput remain separately labeled non-comparative evidence. Repository state cannot transition from the exact withdrawal unless archive inventory, provenance, statistics, digests, bounded presentation text, selected path, and Markdown bytes all revalidate.

## Last full validation

`NSASS-011-META-MATH-UNARY-FUNCTION-INVOCATION` is verified at local implementation checkpoint `798a3d8359b539c69abc4b396cf9c019fc9a6752`. Fresh Dart Sass 1.101.0 probes match native SCSS and indented-Sass `meta.call()` invocation for `abs`, `ceil`, `floor`, `round`, and `percentage`, including default/custom/unprefixed module ownership, true legacy-global origins, canonical positional/named `$number` binding, list/map splats, numeric units, percentage conversion, and once-only source order. Missing, extra, unknown, duplicate, non-number, and unitful-percentage bindings fail closed; `math.compatible` and every remaining callable family remain unavailable. The focused test-first Debug baseline stopped at 307/310 before final Debug/ReleaseSafe each passed 23/23 steps and 310/310 tests. Direct evaluator coverage passes 177/177; full compiler Debug/ReleaseSafe each pass 60/60 steps and 805/805 tests; evaluator cross-compiles pass for Windows x64 GNU and Linux ARM64 GNU in both modes; native contract 10/10 plus check, provider/reference/product 151/151, package policy 6/6 plus check, runner selftest 58/58, formatting, syntax, JSON, and diff gates pass. The SDK selector was removed; production reachability, release state, and external systems are unchanged.

`NSASS-011-META-EXISTENCE-FUNCTION-INVOCATION` is verified at local implementation checkpoint `272dc9fa7c52e01bb4ca598175d4038df1ea323a`. Fresh Dart Sass 1.101.0 probes match native SCSS and indented-Sass `meta.call()` invocation for all five `sass:meta` existence-query references, including default/custom/unprefixed module ownership, true legacy-global origins, independent feature deprecation, canonical positional/named/optional binding, list/map splats, lexical call-site scope, normalized names, loaded-module reflection, and once-only source order.
